\bar{s}\in\{\bar{s}_{h},\cdots,\bar{s}_{H}\}
such that

\displaystyle\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(\bar{s}\mid\pi)-\widehat{R}(\bar{s},\pi)-V_{\mathsf{mc}}(\widehat{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\bar{s},\pi)\mid\pi)\right\rvert\geq\frac{4\varepsilon}{H^{2}},

so therefore
line
16
is executed at least once, proving the first implication.

To prove the second implication, consider any
(\bar{s},\pi)
for which
line
16
is executed. We know that

\displaystyle\left\lvert V^{\pi}(\bar{s})-\widehat{R}(\bar{s},\pi)-V^{\pi}(\widehat{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\bar{s},\pi))\right\rvert\geq\frac{2\varepsilon}{H^{2}}.

Recall that for all
(s,a)
, the estimation error on the rewards was
\lvert R(s,a)-\widehat{R}(s,a)\rvert\leq\varepsilon/H^{2}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\left\lvert V^{\pi}(\bar{s})-R(\bar{s},\pi)-V^{\pi}(\widehat{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\bar{s},\pi))\right\rvert\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{H^{2}},\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\widehat{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\bar{s},\pi)\neq P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\bar{s},\pi).

Therefore as claimed we always delete
\widehat{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\bar{s},\pi)\neq P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\bar{s},\pi)
in
line
16
of
Refit.D
.
∎

Appendix F
Proof of Main Upper Bound

In this section, we prove
Theorem
4
.

F.1
Preliminaries

We will define some additional concepts and notation which will be used in the analysis.

•

For any set
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
we denote the represented states as
\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}^{\prime}]\coloneqq\{\phi(x):x\in\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\}
. For any latent state
s\in\mathcal{S}
and subset
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
we let
n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\prime}]\coloneqq\lvert\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\prime}:\phi(x)=s\}\rvert
count the total number of observations there are emitted from
s
.

•

We define the set of
\varepsilon
-pushforward-reachable latent states

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{h}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\coloneqq\left\{s_{h}:\max_{s_{h-1},a_{h-1}}P_{\mathsf{lat}}(s_{h}\mid s_{h-1},a_{h-1})\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{S}\right\},

and furthermore let
\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\coloneqq\cup_{h=1}^{H}\mathcal{S}_{h}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}
.

•

For any
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
, we let
n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathcal{X}^{\prime}]\coloneqq\lvert\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\prime}:\phi(x)=\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\}\rvert
and
n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathcal{X}^{\prime}]=\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\rvert-n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathcal{X}^{\prime}]
.

Estimated Transitions and Projected Measures.

Recall that the ground truth latent transition is denoted
P_{\mathsf{lat}}:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\to\Delta(\mathcal{S})
. We will use
\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}
to denote the empirical version of the latent transition which is sampled in
line
3
of
Algorithm
5
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid\phi(x_{h}),a_{h})=\frac{1}{n_{\mathsf{dec}}}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{D}}\delta_{\phi(x)}.

In addition, we introduce a notion of projected measures which will be used to relate the ground truth transition
P=\psi\circ P_{\mathsf{lat}}
with the estimated transition
\widehat{P}
of the policy emulator. While our algorithm never directly uses the projected measure, we track it in the analysis.

Definition 13

(Projected Measure)
.

For a distribution
p\in\Delta(\mathcal{S})
, define the
projected measure
onto the observation set
\bar{\mathcal{X}}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
as

\displaystyle\mathsf{Proj}_{\bar{\mathcal{X}}}(p)\coloneqq\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}}p(s)\cdot\mathrm{Unif}(\{x\in\bar{\mathcal{X}}:\phi(x)=s\}).

Specifically, for any
x\in\bar{\mathcal{X}}
we have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Proj}_{\bar{\mathcal{X}}}(p)(x)=p(\phi(x))\cdot\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\right\}}{n_{\phi(x)}[\bar{\mathcal{X}}]}.

Furthermore, for any subset
\bar{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}\subseteq\bar{\mathcal{X}}
we denote
\mathsf{Proj}_{\bar{\mathcal{X}}}(p)(\bar{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime})=\sum_{x\in\bar{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}}\mathsf{Proj}_{\bar{\mathcal{X}}}(p)(x)
.

Formally,
\mathsf{Proj}_{\bar{\mathcal{X}}}
is not a true probability distribution, as the total measure might not sum up to 1. This would happen if
p(s)>0
for
s\in(\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}})^{c}
.

Remark.
In
Theorem
4
, we assume that the distribution
\mu
is factorizable. This can be removed with some extra work. One can modify the definition of the projected measure to replace the uniform distribution over observations with some other suitable importance-reweighted distribution; the existence of such distribution with desirable properties that allow concentration of the pushforward policies can be shown using pushforward concentrability (i.e., in
Lemma
21
).

Test Policy Validity.

In our analysis, we will modify
Definition
12
as below.

Definition 14

(Valid Test Policy)
.

For a layer
h\in[H]
, we say a collection of policies
\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{h}=\left\{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}\right\}_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]}

is a
\eta
-
valid test policy set
for policy emulator
\widehat{M}
if the following hold.

•

(Maximally distinguishing):
\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}=\argmax_{\pi\in\mathcal{A}\circ\Pi_{h+1:H}}\left\lvert\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert
.

•

(Accurate): For all
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]
:

\displaystyle\left\lvert V^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x)-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\eta\quad\text{and}\quad\left\lvert V^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x^{\prime})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert\leq\eta.

F.2
Supporting Technical Lemmas for Sampling

In this section, we establish several technical lemmas which show that various conditions that we need in the analysis hold with high probability under samples from
M
.

Properties of Policy Emulator Initialization.

We prove several properties that hold with high probability when the policy emulator is initialized in
line
4
-
8
of
Algorithm
4
.

Lemma 20

(Sampling of Pushforward-Reachable States)
.

With probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle\forall\penalty\ h\in[H],\forall\penalty\ s\in\mathcal{S}_{h}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}:\quad n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]]\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{2C_{\mathsf{push}}S}\cdot n_{\mathsf{reset}}.

Proof.

Fix any
s\in\mathcal{S}_{h}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}
. For any
i\in[n_{\mathsf{reset}}]
, let
Z^{(i)}
be the indicator variable of whether observation
x^{(i)}_{h}\sim\mu_{h}
satisfies
\phi(x^{(i)}_{h})=s
. We know that
\En[Z^{(i)}]\geq\varepsilon/(C_{\mathsf{push}}S)
. By Chernoff bounds we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\frac{1}{n_{\mathsf{reset}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\mathsf{reset}}}Z^{(i)}\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{\varepsilon}{C_{\mathsf{push}}S}\right]\leq\exp\left(-\frac{n_{\mathsf{reset}}\cdot\varepsilon}{8C_{\mathsf{push}}S}\right),

so as long as

\displaystyle n_{\mathsf{reset}}\geq\frac{8C_{\mathsf{push}}S}{\varepsilon}\log\frac{SH}{\delta},

by union bound, the conclusion of the lemma holds.
∎

Lemma 21

(Pushforward Policy Concentration over
\mu
)
.

Suppose that the conclusion of
Lemma
20
holds. Then with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle\forall\penalty\ h\in[H],\forall\penalty\ (s,a)\in\mathcal{S}_{h}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\times\mathcal{A}:\quad\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\penalty\ \left\lvert\left[\pi_{\sharp}\psi(s)\right](a)-\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]:\phi(x)=s\})\right](a)\right\rvert\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{A}.

Proof.

Fix any
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}_{h}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\times\mathcal{A}
. Also fix any policy
\pi\in\Pi
. Denote the set
\mathcal{X}_{s}=\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]:\phi(x)=s\}
, and observe that
\mathcal{X}_{s}
is drawn i.i.d. from the emission distribution
\psi(s)
. By Hoeffding bounds we have

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\Pr\left[\left\lvert\left[\pi_{\sharp}\psi(s)\right](a)-\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]:\phi(x)=s\})\right](a)\right\rvert\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{A}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{2n_{s}[\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]]\varepsilon^{2}}{A^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{n_{\mathsf{reset}}\varepsilon^{3}}{C_{\mathsf{push}}SA^{2}}\right),

(
Lemma
20
)

Applying union bound we see that as long as

\displaystyle n_{\mathsf{reset}}\geq\frac{C_{\mathsf{push}}SA^{2}}{\varepsilon^{3}}\cdot\log\frac{2SAH\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}

the conclusion of the lemma holds.
∎

Lemma 22

(Sampling Rewards)
.

With probability at least
1-\delta
, every reward estimate
\widehat{R}(x,a)
computed in
line
7
of
Algorithm
4
satisfies
\lvert\widehat{R}(x,a)-R(x,a)\rvert\leq\varepsilon/H
.

Proof.

This follows by Hoeffding inequality and union bound over all
n_{\mathsf{reset}}\cdot AH
pairs
(x,a)\in\mathcal{X}[\widehat{M}]\times\mathcal{A}
.
∎

Properties of
Decoder
.

Now we turn to analyzing a single call to
Decoder
.

Lemma 23

(Sampling Transitions)
.

Fix any
(x_{h},a_{h})
for which we call
Decoder
. With probability at least
1-\delta
, the dataset
\mathcal{D}
sampled in
line
3
of
Algorithm
5
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h})-\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h})\right\|_{1}\leq\varepsilon.

Proof.

Every time a dataset
\mathcal{D}
is sampled, by concentration of discrete distributions we have for any
t>0
:

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\left\|P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h})-\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h})\right\|_{1}\geq\sqrt{S}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{\mathsf{dec}}}}+t\right)\right]\leq\exp(-n_{\mathsf{dec}}t^{2}).

Setting the RHS to
\delta
we have that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\left\|P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h})-\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h})\right\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{\frac{S\log(1/\delta)}{n_{\mathsf{dec}}}}.

Therefore as long as

\displaystyle n_{\mathsf{dec}}\geq\frac{S}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\log\frac{1}{\delta},

the conclusion of the lemma holds.
∎

Corollary 1
.

If the conclusion of
Lemma
23
holds, then the proportion of observations from
(\mathcal{S}_{h}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}})^{c}
in
\mathcal{D}
is at most
2\varepsilon
.

Lemma 24

(Pushforward Policy Concentration over Transitions)
.

Fix any
(x_{h},a_{h})
for which we call
Decoder
. With probability at least
1-\delta
, the dataset
\mathcal{D}
sampled in
line
3
of
Algorithm
5
satisfies

\displaystyle\forall\penalty\ s\in\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{D}],\forall\penalty\ a\in\mathcal{A},\forall\penalty\ \pi\in\Pi:\quad\Big|\left[\pi_{\sharp}\psi(s)\right](a)-\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}\left(\left\{x\in\mathcal{D}:\phi(x)=s\right\}\right)\right](a)\Big|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\log(2SA\lvert\Pi\rvert/\delta)}{n_{s}[\mathcal{D}]}}.

Proof.

Fix a particular
s\in\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{D}]
,
a\in\mathcal{A}
, and
\pi\in\Pi
. The set
\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]:\phi(x)=s\}
is drawn i.i.d. from the emission distribution
\psi(s)
. By Hoeffding bounds we have for any
t>0
:

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\Big|\left[\pi_{\sharp}\psi(s)\right](a)-\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}\left(\left\{x\in\mathcal{D}:\phi(x)=s\right\}\right)\right](a)\Big|\geq t\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-2n_{s}[\mathcal{D}]t^{2}\right).

By union bound over all
(s,a)
and
\pi
, with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle\Big|\left[\pi_{\sharp}\psi(s)\right](a)-\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}\left(\left\{x\in\mathcal{D}:\phi(x)=s\right\}\right)\right](a)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\log(2SA\lvert\Pi\rvert/\delta)}{n_{s}[\mathcal{D}]}}

This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 25

(Monte Carlo Estimates for
Decoder
)
.

Fix any
(x_{h},a_{h})
for which we call
Decoder
. With probability at least
1-\delta
, every Monte Carlo estimate
V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x\mid\pi)
computed in
line
6
of
Algorithm
5
satisfies

\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x\mid\pi)-V^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon
.

Proof.

By Hoeffding’s inequality we know that for a fixed
(x,\pi)
pair:

\displaystyle\Pr\left[\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x\mid\pi)-V^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert\geq\varepsilon\right]\leq 2\exp(-2n_{\mathsf{mc}}\varepsilon^{2}).

In total, we call
line
6
at most
\lvert\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]\rvert^{2}\leq n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{2}
times. Therefore, by union bound, as long as

\displaystyle n_{\mathsf{mc}}\geq K\cdot\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\log\frac{C_{\mathsf{push}}SAH\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\varepsilon\delta}

where
K>0
is an absolute constant determined by the value of
n_{\mathsf{reset}}
, then the result holds.
∎

Properties of
Refit
.

Now we establish the accuracy of estimates in a single call to
Refit
.

Lemma 26

(Monte Carlo Estimates for
Refit
)
.

With probability at least
1-\delta
, every Monte Carlo estimate computed by
Refit
(
line
5
and
11
of
Algorithm
6
) is accurate up to error
\varepsilon
.

Proof.

In
Refit
we compute Monte Carlo estimates for
2n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{2}+2n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{3}\cdot AH
times, since there are
2n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{2}
possible certificates
(x,\pi)
and for each one we perform Monte Carlo estimates over all of the
(\bar{x},\bar{a})
pairs in our policy emulator
\widehat{M}
. By Hoeffding bound and union bound we see that as long as

\displaystyle n_{\mathsf{mc}}\geq K\cdot\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\log\frac{C_{\mathsf{push}}SAH\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\varepsilon\delta},

for some absolute constant
K>0
, the conclusion of the lemma holds.
∎

Additional Notation.

Henceforth, let us define several events:

•

\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{init}}\coloneqq\left\{\text{the conclusions of \hyperref@@ii[lem:sampling-states]{Lemma\penalty\ \ref*{lem:sampling-states}} --- \ref{lem:sampling-rewards} hold}\right\}
. We have
\Pr[\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{init}}]\geq 1-3\delta
.

•

\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{D}}_{t}\coloneqq\left\{\text{the conclusions of \hyperref@@ii[lem:sampling-transitions]{Lemma\penalty\ \ref*{lem:sampling-transitions}} --- \ref{lem:monte-carlo} hold on the $t$-th call to {{Decoder}}{}}\right\}
. We have
\Pr[\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{D}}_{t}]\geq 1-3\delta
. Furthermore, define the random variable
T_{\mathsf{D}}
to be the total number of times that
Decoder
is called.

•

\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{R}}_{t}\coloneqq\left\{\text{the conclusion of \hyperref@@ii[lem:refit-monte-carlo-accuracy]{Lemma\penalty\ \ref*{lem:refit-monte-carlo-accuracy}} holds on the $t$-th call to {{Refit}}{}}\right\}
. We have
\Pr[\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{R}}_{t}]\geq 1-\delta
. Furthermore, define the random variable
T_{\mathsf{R}}
to be the total number of times that
Refit
is called.

In the analysis, we will drop the subscript
t
when referring to
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{D}}_{t}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{R}}_{t}
if clear from the context.

F.3
Analysis of
Decoder

This section is dedicated to establishing
Lemma
27
, which is the main inductive lemma.

Lemma 27

(Induction for
Decoder
)
.

Fix any layer
h\in[H]
and tuple
(x_{h},a_{h})
on which
Decoder
is called. Assume that:

•

\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{init}}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{D}}
hold.

•

For all
x\in\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]
:
\max_{\pi\in\Pi_{h+1:H}}\penalty\ \lvert V^{\pi}(x)-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\rvert\leq\Gamma_{h+1}
.

•

Input confidence set
\mathcal{P}(x_{h},a_{h})
satisfies
\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h}))\in\mathcal{P}(x_{h},a_{h})
.

•

\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{h+1}
are
\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}
-valid test policies for the policy emulator
\widehat{M}
.

Then
Decoder
returns confidence set
\mathcal{P}
via Eq. (
6
) such that:

1.

\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x_{h},a_{h}))\in\mathcal{P}
;

2.

\max_{\bar{p}\in\mathcal{P}}\max_{\pi\in\Pi_{h+1:H}}\lvert Q^{\pi}(x_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{R}(x_{h},a_{h})-\En_{x\sim\bar{p}}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\rvert\leq\Gamma_{h+1}+K\cdot\left(\beta+S\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}\right)
.

Here,
K>0
is an absolute numerical constant.

F.3.1
Structural Properties of the Decoder Graph

For the lemmas in this section, we will assume the preconditions of
Lemma
27
and analyze properties of the decoder graph
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{obs}}
constructed in a single call to
Decoder
.

Lemma 28

(Validity of Decoding Function)
.

Under the preconditions of
Lemma
27
, for every
x_{l}\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}
, we have

\displaystyle\{x_{r}\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}:\phi(x_{r})=\phi(x_{l})\}\subseteq\mathcal{T}[x_{l}].

Proof.

The proof is a reprise of the argument used in Part (1) of
Lemma
18
. We prove this by contradiction. Suppose that there existed some
x_{l}\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}
and
x_{r}\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}
such that
\phi(x_{l})=\phi(x_{r})
but
x_{r}\notin\mathcal{T}[x_{l}]
. Then
x_{r}
must have lost a test to some other
x_{r}^{\prime}
, i.e. there exists some
x_{r}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]
such that

\displaystyle\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x_{l}\mid\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r})\right\rvert\geq\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+2\varepsilon.

(39)

By accuracy of
\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{h+1}
and the fact that
\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}
is open-loop at layer
h+1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert V^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{l})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r})\right\rvert=\left\lvert V^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r})\right\rvert\leq\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}.

(40)

Furthermore, by
Lemma
25
we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x_{l}\mid\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}})-V^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r})\right\rvert=\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x_{l}\mid\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}})-V^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{l})\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon.

(41)

Combining (
40
) and (
41
) we get that

\displaystyle\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x_{l}\mid\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r})\right\rvert\leq\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+\varepsilon,

which contradicts (
39
). This proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma 29

(Biclique Property)
.

Under the preconditions of
Lemma
27
, for any
s\in\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}]
the subgraph of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{obs}}
over vertices
\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\cup\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}:\phi(x)=s\}
is a biclique.

Proof.

Fix any
s\in\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}]
. By
Lemma
28
, any
x_{l}\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}
such that
\phi(x_{l})=s
has an edge to every observation
\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}:\phi(x)=s\}
in
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{obs}}
. Therefore, the subgraph over
\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{L}}\cup\mathcal{X}_{\mathsf{R}}:\phi(x)=s\}
forms a biclique in
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{obs}}
.
∎

Lemma 30
.

Under the preconditions of
Lemma
27
, for any connected component
\mathbb{C}
,
\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\subseteq\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]
.

Proof.

Fix any
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]
, and let
x_{l}\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}
be any arbitrary observation such that
\phi(x_{l})=s
. By
Lemma
20
, since
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}
, there exist some
x_{r}\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}
such that
\phi(x_{r})=s
; in other words,
s\in\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}]
. Moreover by
Lemma
28
, there must be an edge from
x_{l}
to
x_{r}
in
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{obs}}
. Therefore
x_{r}\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}
, so
s\in\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]
. ∎

Lemma 31
.

Let
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}
such that
\phi(x)=\phi(x^{\prime})
. We have
\max_{a\in\mathcal{A},\pi\in\Pi}\penalty\ \lvert\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x,a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x^{\prime},a)\rvert\leq 2\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}
.

Proof.

Denote
\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}=\argmax_{\pi\in\mathcal{A}\circ\Pi}\penalty\ \lvert\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\rvert
to be the test policy for the pair
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}
. By accuracy of the test policy we know that

\displaystyle\left\lvert V^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x)-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\lvert V^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x^{\prime})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert\leq\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}.

Furthermore since
x,x^{\prime}
are observations emitted from the same latent state and
\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}
is open loop at layer
h+1
, we have
V^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x)=V^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x^{\prime})
. Therefore

\displaystyle\max_{a\in\mathcal{A},\pi\in\Pi}\penalty\ \left\lvert\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x,a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x^{\prime},a)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\widehat{Q}^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x,\pi_{x,x^{\prime}})-\widehat{Q}^{\pi_{x,x^{\prime}}}(x^{\prime},\pi_{x,x^{\prime}})\right\rvert\leq 2\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}.

This concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 32
.

Fix any
x_{l}\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}
. If
x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}[x_{l}]
, then
\max_{a\in\mathcal{A},\pi\in\Pi}\penalty\ \lvert\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{r},a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{r}^{\prime},a)\rvert\leq 2\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+4\varepsilon
.

Proof.

By definition of
\mathcal{T}[x_{l}]
we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x_{l}\mid\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r})\right\rvert\leq\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+2\varepsilon\quad\text{and}\quad\left\lvert V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x_{l}\mid\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r}^{\prime})\right\rvert\leq\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+2\varepsilon.

Using the fact that test policies are maximally distinguishing we have

\displaystyle\max_{a\in\mathcal{A},\pi\in\Pi}\penalty\ \left\lvert\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{r},a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{r}^{\prime},a)\right\rvert=\left\lvert\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r})-\widehat{V}^{\pi_{x_{r},x_{r}^{\prime}}}(x_{r}^{\prime})\right\rvert\leq 2\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+4\varepsilon.

This proves the lemma.
∎

Lemma 33

(Bounded Width of
\mathbb{C}
)
.

For any connected component
\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}_{j\geq 1}
in
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{obs}}
we have

\displaystyle\max_{x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\max_{a\in\mathcal{A},\pi\in\Pi}\penalty\ \lvert\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x,a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x^{\prime},a)\rvert\leq 4S\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+8S\varepsilon.

Proof.

Let us take any
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}
. Since
x,x^{\prime}
belong to the same connected component, there exists a sequence of observations
\mathsf{seq}=(x_{1}=x,\dots,x_{n}=x^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})^{n}
such that for every consecutive pair
x_{i},x_{i+1}
there exists some
x_{l}\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}
such that
x_{i},x_{i+1}\in\mathcal{T}[x_{l}]
.

Fix any
a\in\mathcal{A},\pi\in\Pi
. Now we will bound
\lvert\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x,a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x^{\prime},a)\rvert
. We construct an auxiliary sequence
\widetilde{\mathsf{seq}}=(\widetilde{x}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{x}_{k})
for some
k\leq n
as follows:

•

Initialize
\widetilde{\mathsf{seq}}=\emptyset
.

•

For
i=1,\cdots,n
:

–

Add
x_{i}
to the end of
\widetilde{\mathsf{seq}}
.

–

If there exists
x_{j}
with
j>i
such that
\phi(x_{i})=\phi(x_{j})
then set
i\leftarrow j
.

Observe that
\widetilde{\mathsf{seq}}
satisfies the following conditions:

•

\widetilde{x}_{1}=x
and
\widetilde{x}_{k}=x^{\prime}
.

•

For every
s\in\mathcal{S}
, at most two observations
\widetilde{x},\widetilde{x}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{supp}(\psi(s))\cap\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}
are found in
\widetilde{\mathsf{seq}}
, and these observations must appear sequentially.

•

For any
i\in[k-1]
, if
\phi(\widetilde{x}_{i})\neq\phi(\widetilde{x}_{i+1})
then there exists some
x_{l}\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}
such that
\widetilde{x}_{i},\widetilde{x}_{i+1}\in\mathcal{T}[x_{l}]
.

Now we can apply triangle inequality to
\widetilde{\mathsf{seq}}
:

\displaystyle\left\lvert\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x,a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x^{\prime},a)\right\rvert\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left\lvert\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(\widetilde{x}_{i},a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(\widetilde{x}_{i+1},a)\right\rvert\leq 4S\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+8S\varepsilon.

The final bound uses the aforementioned properties of
\widetilde{\mathsf{seq}}
, as well as
Lemma
31
and
Lemma
32
to handle the individual terms in the summation. This completes the proof of
Lemma
33
.
∎

F.3.2
Structural Properties of Projected Measures

Now we will prove several lemmas regarding the projected measure of the empirical latent distribution

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}=\frac{1}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}}\delta_{\phi(x)}.

which is sampled in
line
3
of a single call to
Decoder
.

Lemma 34
.

Under the preconditions of
Lemma
27
, for any connected component
\mathbb{C}
of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{obs}}
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(s).

Proof.

We compute that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{n_{\phi(x)}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\right\}}{n_{\phi(x)}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}}{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}]}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}}{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}]}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}}{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}]}.

For
(i)
, observe that if
s\notin\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]
, then the sum
\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}}{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}]}=0
. For
(ii)
, we use the fact that
n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]=0
if
s\notin\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]
. From here, we apply the biclique lemma (
Lemma
29
). The biclique lemma implies that if
s\in\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]
, then
\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}:\phi(x)=s\}\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}
, and therefore
\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]=\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]
. Furthermore for any
s\in\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]
, all of the observations
\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}:\phi(x)=s\}\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}
, so
n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}]=n_{s}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]
. Thus we can continue the calculation as

\displaystyle\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}}{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}}{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(s).

This concludes the proof of
Lemma
34
.
∎

Corollary 2
.

Under the preconditions of
Lemma
27
, then

\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}(\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}))\in[0,2\varepsilon]
.

Proof.

For any
\mathbb{C}
we have

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}

(
Lemma
34
)

\displaystyle=\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}

(
Lemma
30
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}+\sum_{s\in(\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}})^{c}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}-\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in(\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}})^{c}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}.

The last equality uses the fact that by
Lemma
30
,
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\Rightarrow s\in\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]
, so in particular by
Lemma
29
we have
\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}:\phi(x)=s\}\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}
, so therefore
n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]=n_{s}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]
.

Summing over all
\mathbb{C}
and applying
Corollary
1
we get that

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})=\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\sum_{s\in(\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}})^{c}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}=\sum_{s\in(\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}})^{c}}\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\in[0,2\varepsilon].

This proves
Corollary
2
.
∎

Lemma 35
.

Under the preconditions of
Lemma
27
, for every
\pi\in\Pi
and
\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}
:

\displaystyle\max_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\penalty\ \left\lvert\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right](a)-\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}})\right](a)\right\rvert\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{A}+K\cdot\sqrt{\frac{S\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}}+\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]},

where
K>0
is an absolute constant.

Proof.

Fix any
\pi\in\Pi
,
\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}
, and
a\in\mathcal{A}
. We can calculate that:

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right](a)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{n_{\phi(x)}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}}{n_{\phi(x)}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}}\left(\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\frac{1}{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\cdot\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\left(\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\frac{1}{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\cdot\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\left(\frac{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\frac{1}{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\cdot\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\left(\frac{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\frac{1}{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\cdot\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right).

The last line uses the biclique lemma (
Lemma
29
) in the same fashion as the proof of
Lemma
34
. Now we apply the conclusions of
Lemma
21
and
Lemma
24
, along with the fact that for every
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]
we have
\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}:\phi(x)=s\}\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}
as well as
\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}:\phi(x)=s\}\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}
(which is again implied by the biclique lemma):

\displaystyle\hskip-40.00006pt\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right](a)

\displaystyle\leq\quad

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left(\left[\pi_{\sharp}\psi(s)\right](a)+\frac{\varepsilon}{A}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\quad

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\frac{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\Bigg(\frac{\varepsilon}{A}+\sqrt{\frac{2\log\tfrac{2SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}}

\displaystyle\hskip 200.0003pt+\frac{1}{n_{s}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\cdot\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\Bigg(\frac{\varepsilon}{A}\cdot n_{s}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]+\sqrt{2n_{s}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\log\tfrac{2SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}

\displaystyle\hskip 200.0003pt+\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\Bigg)

By
Lemma
34
, we have
\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]=n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]
. Using Cauchy-Schwarz we get that

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right](a)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{A}+K\cdot\sqrt{\frac{S\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}}+\frac{1}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon}{A}+K\cdot\sqrt{\frac{S\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}}+\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\cdot\frac{1}{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right)

(42)

Let us investigate the last term. We have

\displaystyle\hskip-40.00006pt\frac{1}{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right)

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right)

(
Lemma
30
)

\displaystyle\leq\quad

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+\frac{1}{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{s\in(\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}})^{c}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\left(\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right)

\displaystyle=\quad

\displaystyle\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}})\right](a)

Plugging this back into Eq. (
42
) we get that

\displaystyle\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right](a)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{A}+K\cdot\sqrt{\frac{S\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}}+\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\cdot\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}})\right](a),

and rearranging and using the fact that
\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}})\right](a)\in[0,1]
we get that

\displaystyle\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right](a)-\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}})\right](a)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{A}+K\cdot\sqrt{\frac{S\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}}+\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}.

One can repeat the same steps to get the lower bound. Therefore,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right](a)-\left[\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}})\right](a)\right\rvert\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{A}+K\cdot\sqrt{\frac{S\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}}+\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}.

This proves
Lemma
35
.
∎

F.3.3
Proof of
Lemma
27

Fix the
(x_{h},a_{h})
pair on which we call
Decoder
.

For notational convenience we will denote
\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\coloneqq\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}\coloneqq\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]
, as well as use
P_{\mathsf{lat}}=P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid\phi(x_{h}),a_{h})
to denote the ground truth latent transition function. Throughout the proof, we use
K>0
to denote absolute constants whose values may change line-by-line.

Part (1).

Since the input confidence set
\mathcal{P}
satisfies the third bullet, it suffices to show that that
\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})
satisfies both of the constraints in the confidence set construction of Eq. (
6
).

For the first constraint, observe that by
Corollary
2
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\left\lvert\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right\rvert\leq 2\varepsilon.

Therefore it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\left\lvert\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon.

We calculate that

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\left\lvert\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}\left\lvert\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}\left\lvert\left(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\phi(x))-\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\phi(x))\right)\cdot\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\right\}}{n_{\phi(x)}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}}\sum_{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}\left\lvert\left(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(s)-\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(s)\right)\cdot\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)=s\right\}}{n_{s}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}}\left\lvert P_{\mathsf{lat}}(s)-\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(s)\right\rvert\leq\varepsilon.

(
Lemma
23
)

(43)

Now we prove that
\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})
also satisfies the second constraint, i.e.,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}\leq\varepsilon.

Observe that we can break up the bound as follows:

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}}_{\eqqcolon\mathtt{Term}_{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+\underbrace{\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}}_{\eqqcolon\mathtt{Term}_{2}}.

Bounding
\mathtt{Term}_{1}
.
To bound
\mathtt{Term}_{1}
, we compute:

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}+\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}<4\varepsilon}\frac{2\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}+(8S+4)\varepsilon

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left(K\cdot\sqrt{\frac{SA^{2}\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}}+A\cdot\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\right)+(8S+5)\varepsilon

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon}\frac{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]+n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left(K\cdot\sqrt{\frac{SA^{2}\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}}+A\cdot\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\right)+(8S+5)\varepsilon.

(44)

The inequality
(i)
follows by casework on
\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}
:

•

If
\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\neq\emptyset
then by the biclique lemma (
Lemma
29
) we have
\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}:\phi(x)\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\}\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}
. In other words, all of the observations from states in
\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]
are contained in this
\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}
. Therefore, there can be at most
S
such components
\mathbb{C}
, and their contribution to the sum is
8\varepsilon\cdot S
.

•

If
\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]=\emptyset
, then
\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}
only contains observations from
(\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}})^{c}
, and therefore the total size of such
\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}
can be bounded by
2\varepsilon\cdot\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert
using
Corollary
1
. Their contribution to the sum is
4\varepsilon
.

Furthermore,
(ii)
uses
Lemma
35
.

We now proceed to separately bound the terms in Eq. (
44
). First, observe that

\displaystyle K\sqrt{SA^{2}\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}\cdot\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon}\frac{\sqrt{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq K\sqrt{\frac{S^{2}A^{2}\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}]}}

\displaystyle\leq K\sqrt{\frac{S^{2}A^{2}\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{dec}}}}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon.

(45)

The first inequality follows because by the biclique lemma (
Lemma
29
) we know that the summation must have at most
S
terms, since each of the
\mathbb{C}
contains some
s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}
, so we can apply Cauchy-Schwarz for
S
-dimensional vectors. The second inequality is a consequence of
Corollary
1
, and the last inequality follows by our choice of
n_{\mathsf{dec}}
.

In addition by
Corollary
1
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon}\frac{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\leq 2\varepsilon.

(46)

For the other two terms, observe that by
Lemma
35
, when
\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon
we must have
\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\leq 1
so therefore

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon}\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\left(K\cdot\sqrt{\frac{SA^{2}\log\tfrac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}}+\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon}\frac{n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\left(K\cdot\sqrt{SA^{2}\log\frac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}+1\right)

\displaystyle\leq K\sqrt{SA^{2}\log\frac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}\cdot\varepsilon.

(47)

Combining Eqns. (
44
), (
45
), (
46
), and (
47
) we get that

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}\leq K\left(\sqrt{SA^{2}\log\frac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}+S\right)\varepsilon.

(48)

Bounding
\mathtt{Term}_{2}
.
To bound
\mathtt{Term}_{2}
, fix any
\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}
. Note that

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left\lvert\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\left(\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}-\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\right)\mathbbm{1}\{\pi(x)=a\}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\left\lvert\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}-\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\left\lvert\frac{\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(\phi(x))}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}-\frac{P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\phi(x))}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\right\rvert\cdot\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{\phi(x)\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\right\}}{n_{\phi(x)}[\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\left\lvert\frac{\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(s)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}-\frac{P_{\mathsf{lat}}(s)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}\left\lvert\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}(s)-P_{\mathsf{lat}}(s)\cdot\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt+\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]\cap\mathcal{S}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}]}P_{\mathsf{lat}}(s)\left\lvert 1-\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\right\rvert

(
Lemma
23
)

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}+\frac{1}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\left\lvert\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\varepsilon}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}=2\varepsilon\frac{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}.

(using Eq.
(
43
)
)

Also, we have the trivial bound that
\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}\leq 2
, because it is a difference of two probability measures, so we can write the bound

\displaystyle\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}\leq 2\varepsilon\frac{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\wedge 2.

(49)

Using Eq.
(
49
)
we get that

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left(\frac{\varepsilon\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\wedge 1\right)=2\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\left(\frac{\varepsilon\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\wedge\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon}\frac{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]+n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}+2\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}:\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}<4\varepsilon}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\leq(8S+8)\varepsilon.

(50)

The last inequality uses the facts that (1)
Corollary
1
implies that for any
\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}
such that
\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\geq 4\varepsilon
we have
\frac{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]+n_{\mathsf{unreach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}{n_{\mathsf{reach}}[\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}]}\leq 2
and (2) the same casework we showed above to handle the summation for
\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}
such that
\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}<4\varepsilon
.

Putting together Eqns. (
48
) and (
50
):

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq K\left(\sqrt{SA^{2}\log\frac{SA\lvert\Pi\rvert}{\delta}}+S\right)\varepsilon\eqqcolon\beta.

Thus, we can conclude that
\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})\in\mathcal{P}
, thus concluding the proof of Part (1).

Part (2).

Observe that in light of Part (1), the set
\mathcal{P}
is nonempty so therefore the maximum is well defined.

We want to show a bound on

\displaystyle\max_{\bar{p}\in\mathcal{P}}\max_{\pi\in\Pi_{h+1:H}}\penalty\ \left\lvert Q^{\pi}(x_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{R}(x_{h},a_{h})-\En_{x\sim\bar{p}}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert.

Fix any
\bar{p}\in\mathcal{P}
and
\pi\in\Pi_{h+1:H}
. We compute

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\left\lvert Q^{\pi}(x_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{R}(x_{h},a_{h})-\En_{x\sim\bar{p}}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{H}+\left\lvert\En_{s\sim P_{\mathsf{lat}}}V^{\pi}(s)-\En_{s\sim\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}}V^{\pi}(s)\right\rvert+\left\lvert\En_{s\sim\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}}V^{\pi}(s)-\En_{x\sim\bar{p}}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert

(
Lemma
22
)

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon+\left\lvert\En_{s\sim\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}}V^{\pi}(s)-\En_{x\sim\bar{p}}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert

(
Lemma
23
)

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon+\underbrace{\left\lvert\En_{s\sim\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}}V^{\pi}(s)-\En_{x\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert}_{\eqqcolon\mathtt{Term}_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\left\lvert\En_{x\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x)-\En_{x\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert}_{\eqqcolon\mathtt{Term}_{2}}+\underbrace{\left\lvert\En_{x\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)-\En_{x\sim\bar{p}}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert}_{\eqqcolon\mathtt{Term}_{3}}.

Bounding
\mathtt{Term}_{1}
.
For the first term, we can calculate that

\displaystyle\mathtt{Term}_{1}

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\En_{s\sim\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}}V^{\pi}(s)-\En_{x\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\En_{s\sim\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}}\En_{x\sim\psi(s)}V^{\pi}(x)-\En_{x\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\En_{s\sim\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}}\left[\En_{x\sim\psi(s)}V^{\pi}(x)-\En_{x\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}:\phi(x)=s\})}V^{\pi}(x)\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon+\left\lvert\En_{s\sim\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}}}\left[\mathbbm{1}\left\{s\in\mathcal{S}_{h}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\right\}\left(\En_{x\sim\psi(s)}V^{\pi}(x)-\En_{x\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\{x\in\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}:\phi(x)=s\})}V^{\pi}(x)\right)\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 3\varepsilon.

(51)

The first inequality follows by
Corollary
1
, and the second inequality follows by
Lemma
21
.

Bounding
\mathtt{Term}_{2}
.
For the second term, we have by assumption that:

\displaystyle\mathtt{Term}_{2}=\left\lvert\En_{x\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})}\left[V^{\pi}(x)-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right]\right\rvert\leq\Gamma_{h+1}.

(52)

Bounding
\mathtt{Term}_{3}
.
Now we calculate a bound on
\mathtt{Term}_{3}
. In what follows for any connected component
\mathbb{C}
we let
x_{\mathbb{C}}
denote an arbitrary fixed observation from
\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}
(for example, the lowest indexed one). Observe that for any
p\in\Delta(\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}})
we have

\displaystyle\En_{x\sim p}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}p(x)\cdot\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x,\pi(x))

(
\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}
form a partition of
\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}
)

\displaystyle\leq 4S\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+8S\varepsilon+\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}p(x)\cdot\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x_{\mathbb{C}}))

(
Lemma
33
)

\displaystyle=4S\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+8S\varepsilon+\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}p(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{p(x)}{p(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x_{\mathbb{C}})).

Similarly, one can show the lower bound on
\En_{x\sim p}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)
. Therefore we apply the bound to get:

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\left\lvert\En_{x\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)-\En_{x\sim\bar{p}}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 8S\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+16S\varepsilon

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\left\lvert\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x))-\bar{p}(\mathbb{C})\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}}}\frac{\bar{p}(x)}{\bar{p}(\mathbb{C})}\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x))\right\rvert

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}8S\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+16S\varepsilon+2\varepsilon

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\left\lvert\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}}\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x))-\bar{p}(\mathbb{C})\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}}\frac{\bar{p}(x)}{\bar{p}(\mathbb{C})}\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x))\right\rvert

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}8S\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+16S\varepsilon+5\varepsilon+\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\lvert\sum_{x\in\mathbb{C}}\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x))-\frac{\bar{p}(x)}{\bar{p}(\mathbb{C})}\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x))\right\rvert,

\displaystyle\leq 8S\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+16S\varepsilon+5\varepsilon+\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})-\pi_{\sharp}\bar{p}(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C})\right\|_{1},

where
(i)
follows by
Corollary
2
and the bound
\frac{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(x)}{\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})}\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x))\in[0,1]
, and
(ii)
follows by the first constraint on
\bar{p}\in\mathcal{P}
and the bound
\frac{p(x)}{p(\mathbb{C})}\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x_{\mathbb{C}},\pi(x))\in[0,1]
.

From here, we will use the second constraint on
\bar{p}\in\mathcal{P}
:

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\left\lvert\En_{x\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)-\En_{x\sim\bar{p}}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq 8S\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+16S\varepsilon+5\varepsilon+\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})-\pi_{\sharp}\bar{p}(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C})\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 8S\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+16S\varepsilon+5\varepsilon+\beta+\sum_{\mathbb{C}\in\{\mathbb{C}_{j}\}}\frac{\lvert\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}{\lvert\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{L}}\rvert}\cdot\left\|\pi_{\sharp}\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})(\cdot\mid\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{R}})-\pi_{\sharp}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{C}^{\mathsf{L}})\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 8S\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}+16S\varepsilon+5\varepsilon+2\beta.

(53)

The last inequality follows because our proof for Part (1) of the lemma actually showed that
\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mathsf{R}}}(\widetilde{P}_{\mathsf{lat}})\in\mathcal{P}
. Combining Eqns. (
51
), (
52
), and (
53
) we get the final bound

\displaystyle\left\lvert Q^{\pi}(x_{h},a_{h})-\widehat{R}(x_{h},a_{h})-\En_{x\sim\bar{p}}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert\leq\Gamma_{h+1}+K\cdot\left(\beta+S\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}\right).

This completes the proof of
Lemma
27
.

F.4
Analysis of
Refit

Lemma 36

(Certificate Implies Transition Inaccuracy)
.

Assume that
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{init}}
hold. Let
\widehat{M}
be a policy emulator. Suppose there exists a certificate
(x,\pi)\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]\times(\mathcal{A}\circ\Pi_{h+1:H})
such that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)-V^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert\geq\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}.

Then there exists some tuple
(\bar{x},\bar{a})\in\mathcal{X}[\widehat{M}]\times\mathcal{A}
such that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert\geq\frac{\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{2H}.

(54)

Proof.

Suppose that Eq. (
54
) did not hold for any
(\bar{x},\bar{a})
. Then by the Performance Difference Lemma we have

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\left\lvert V^{\pi}(x)-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\widehat{R}(x,\pi)-R(x,\pi)\right\rvert+\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid x,\pi)}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid x,\pi)}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt+\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid x,\pi)}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid x,\pi)}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\frac{\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{2H}+\frac{\varepsilon}{H}+\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid x,\pi)}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid x,\pi)}\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{2H}+\frac{\varepsilon}{H}+\max_{x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}\left\lvert V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\cdots\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\frac{\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{2}+\varepsilon,

where
(i)
uses
Lemma
22
and the negation of Eq. (
54
), and
(ii)
applies the bound recursively. Since
\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}>2\varepsilon
, we have reached a contradiction. This proves
Lemma
36
.
∎

Lemma 37

(Refitting Correctness)
.

Assume that
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{init}},\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{R}}
hold. The following are true about
Algorithm
6
in the search for incorrect transitions (
line
9
-
15
are executed):

(1)

For every
(x,\pi)
from in
line
10
, at least one such
(\bar{x},\bar{a})
pair is identified by
line
13
.

(2)

Every
(\bar{x},\bar{a})
pair identified by
line
13
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h(\bar{x})+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert\geq\frac{\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{16H}.

(3)

For every
(\bar{x},\bar{a})
identified by
line
13
, the corresponding loss vector
\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}
from
line
15
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\langle\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h(\bar{x})+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})),\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{16H}.

Proof.

To prove Part (1) we use
Lemma
36
, which shows that there exists at least one such
(\bar{x},\bar{a})
such that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert\geq\frac{\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{2H}.

(55)

Therefore we know that for such
(\bar{x},\bar{a})
:

\displaystyle\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x^{\prime}\mid\pi)+\widehat{R}(\bar{x},\bar{a})-Q_{\mathsf{mc}}(\bar{x},\bar{a}\mid\pi)\right\rvert+3\varepsilon

(
Lemma
26
and
Lemma
22
)

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\Delta(\bar{x},\bar{a})\right\rvert+3\varepsilon

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad\left\lvert\Delta(\bar{x},\bar{a})\right\rvert\geq\frac{\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{2H}-3\varepsilon\geq\frac{\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{8H},

(Using Eq. (
55
))

so therefore this
(\bar{x},\bar{a})
is identified by
line
13
of
Refit
.

Now we prove Part (2). Fix any
(\bar{x},\bar{a})
pair identified by
line
13
of
Refit
. Let
h=h(\bar{x})
denote the layer that a given
\bar{x}
is found. First we observe that

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\En_{x^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})=\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\psi\circ P_{\mathsf{lat}}}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\En_{s\sim P_{\mathsf{lat}}}\left[\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\psi(s)}\left[V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right]-\mathbbm{1}\left\{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\right\}\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]:\phi(x)=s\})}\left[V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon+\En_{s\sim P_{\mathsf{lat}}}\left[\mathbbm{1}\left\{s\in\mathcal{S}^{\varepsilon\textsf{-push}}\right\}\left(\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\psi(s)}\left[V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right]-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\{x\in\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]:\phi(x)=s\})}\left[V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right]\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon,

where the last inequality uses
Lemma
21
. The other side of the inequality can be similarly shown, so

\displaystyle\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert\leq 2\varepsilon.

(56)

We can compute that

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{8H}

\displaystyle\leq\lvert\Delta(\bar{x},\bar{a})\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V_{\mathsf{mc}}(x^{\prime}\mid\pi)+\widehat{R}(\bar{x},\bar{a})-Q_{\mathsf{mc}}(\bar{x},\bar{a}\mid\pi)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})+\widehat{R}(\bar{x},\bar{a})-Q^{\pi}(\bar{x},\bar{a})\right\rvert+2\varepsilon

(
Lemma
26
)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim P(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert+3\varepsilon

(
Lemma
22
)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert+5\varepsilon.

(Eq. (
56
))

Rearranging we see that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})\right\rvert\geq\frac{\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{8H}-5\varepsilon\geq\frac{\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{16H},

and this proves Part (2).

For Part (3), suppose that
\Delta(\bar{x},\bar{a})\geq\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}/8H
(the case where
\Delta(\bar{x},\bar{a})\leq-\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}/8H
can be tackled similarly). Then we have
\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}\coloneqq Q_{\mathsf{mc}}(\cdot,\pi(\cdot)\mid\pi)\in[0,1]^{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}
. We can compute that

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{8H}

\displaystyle\leq\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})}Q_{\mathsf{mc}}(x^{\prime},\pi(x^{\prime})\mid\pi)+\widehat{R}(\bar{x},\bar{a})-Q_{\mathsf{mc}}(\bar{x},\bar{a}\mid\pi)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a}),\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}\right\rangle+\widehat{R}(\bar{x},\bar{a})-Q_{\mathsf{mc}}(\bar{x},\bar{a}\mid\pi)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon+\left\langle\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a}),\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}\right\rangle+\widehat{R}(\bar{x},\bar{a})-Q^{\pi}(\bar{x},\bar{a})

(
Lemma
26
)

\displaystyle\leq 4\varepsilon+\left\langle\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a}),\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}\right\rangle-\En_{x^{\prime}\sim\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}})}V^{\pi}(x^{\prime})

(
Lemma
22
and Eq. (
56
))

\displaystyle\leq 5\varepsilon+\left\langle\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})),\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}\right\rangle

(
Lemma
26
)

Rearranging we get
\left\langle\widehat{P}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid\bar{x},\bar{a})),\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}\right\rangle\geq\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}/(16H)
, thus proving part (3).
∎

Lemma 38

(Bound on Number of Refits)
.

Assume that
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{init}},\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{R}}
hold, and that every time
Algorithm
6
is called, the confidence sets
\mathcal{P}
satisfy

\displaystyle\forall\penalty\ (x,a)\in\mathcal{X}[\widehat{M}]\times\mathcal{A}:\quad\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h(x)+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x,a))\in\mathcal{P}(x,a).

Then across all calls to
Algorithm
6
,
line
15
is executed at most
(n_{\mathsf{reset}}AH/\varepsilon^{2})\cdot\log n_{\mathsf{reset}}
times.

Proof.

Fix
h\in[H]
and a pair
(x,a)\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]\times\mathcal{A}
. Suppose we execute
line
15
for
T_{\mathrm{refit}}
times on
(x,a)
. Denote the sequence of transition estimates as
\{\widehat{P}^{(t)}(\cdot\mid x,a)\}_{t\in[T_{\mathrm{refit}}]}
and the sequence of loss vectors as
\{\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}^{(t)}\}_{t\in[T_{\mathrm{refit}}]}
.

By Part (3) of
Lemma
37
, for all times
t\in[T_{\mathrm{refit}}]
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\widehat{P}^{(t)}(\cdot\mid x,a)-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x,a)),\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}^{(t)}\right\rangle\geq\frac{\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}}{16H}.

(57)

On the other hand, we have a bound on the total regret of OMD with step size
\varepsilon

[
11
, see, e.g., Thm. 5.2 of]
:

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003pt\sum_{t=1}^{T_{\mathrm{refit}}}\left\langle\widehat{P}^{(t)}(\cdot\mid x,a)-\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x,a)),\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}^{(t)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon}D_{\mathsf{ne}}\left(\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h+1}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x,a))\penalty\ \|\penalty\ \widehat{P}^{(1)}(\cdot\mid x,a)\right)+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T_{\mathrm{refit}}}\left\|\ell_{\mathrm{loss}}^{(t)}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\log n_{\mathsf{reset}}}{\varepsilon}+\frac{\varepsilon T_{\mathrm{refit}}}{2}.

(58)

Therefore, combining Eqs. (
57
) and (
58
) along with the value
\epsilon_{\mathsf{tol}}=80H\varepsilon
we have the bound

\displaystyle T_{\mathrm{refit}}\leq\frac{\log n_{\mathsf{reset}}}{\varepsilon^{2}}.

Using the fact that there are
