n_{\mathsf{reset}}AH
such
(x,a)
pairs proves the result.
∎

F.5
Proof of
Theorem
4

In the proof, we will assume that
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{init}}
holds, that
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{D}}_{t}
holds for all times
t\in[T_{\mathsf{D}}]
, that
\mathcal{E}^{\mathsf{R}}_{t}
holds for all times
t\in[T_{\mathsf{R}}]
. By union bound, this holds with probability at least
1-(3T_{\mathsf{D}}+T_{\mathsf{R}}+3)\delta
.

We will show that under the choice of parameters
n_{\mathsf{reset}}
,
n_{\mathsf{dec}}
, and
n_{\mathsf{mc}}
in the algorithm pseudocode,
PLHR
returns a
\widetilde{O}(SAH^{2}\varepsilon)
-optimal policy, and that
T_{\mathsf{D}},T_{\mathsf{R}}\leq\mathrm{poly}(C_{\mathsf{push}},S,A,H,\varepsilon^{-1},\log\lvert\Pi\rvert,\log\delta^{-1})
. Therefore, rescaling
\varepsilon
and
\delta
will imply the final result.

Proof by Induction.

Take
\Gamma_{h}\coloneqq K(H-h+1)(\beta+SH)\varepsilon
for some suitably large constant
K>0
. Furthermore set
\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}=81H\varepsilon
. We will show that the following properties holds at every layer
h\in[H]
:

(1)

Transition set includes ground truth:

\forall\penalty\ (x,a)\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]\times\mathcal{A}
,
\mathsf{Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]}(P_{\mathsf{lat}}(\cdot\mid x,a))\in\mathcal{P}(x,a)
.

(2)

Accurate value estimates:

\forall\penalty\ (x,a)\in\mathcal{X}_{h}[\widehat{M}]\times\mathcal{A},\pi\in\Pi_{h+1:H}
,
\lvert Q^{\pi}(x,a)-\widehat{Q}^{\pi}(x,a)\rvert\leq\Gamma_{h}
.

(3)

Valid test policies:

\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{h}
are
\epsilon_{\mathsf{dec}}
-valid for
\widehat{M}
at layer
h
.

To analyze
PLHR
we will show that at the end of every while loop, these properties always hold for all layers
h>\ell_{\mathsf{next}}
.

Base Case.
For the first loop with
\ell=H
, property (1) holds because there are no transitions to be constructed at layer
H
. By
Lemma
22
, property (2) holds after the initialization of the policy emulator in
line
8
. Furthermore, in the first call to
Refit
, the computed test policies are open loop, so again using
Lemma
22
, we see that
line
8
is triggered. Therefore, properties (2) and (3) hold at the end of the while loop. The current layer index is set to
\ell\leftarrow H-1
.

Inductive Step.
Suppose the current layer index is
\ell
, and that properties (1)–(3) hold for all
h>\ell
. By
Lemma
27
, for every
(x_{\ell},a_{\ell})
that we call
Decoder
on the updated confidence sets
\mathcal{P}
returned by satisfy property (1), and the choice
\widehat{P}\in\mathcal{P}
satisfies property (2). Now we do casework on the outcome of
Refit
called at layer
\ell
.

•

Case 1: Return in
line
8
.
By construction, property (3) is satisfied for layer
\ell
. In this case, since
Refit
made no updates to
\widehat{M}_{\mathsf{lat}}
or
\mathcal{P}
, properties (1) and (2) continue to hold at layer
\ell
onwards.

•

Case 2: Return in
line
16
.
Property (1) holds because
Refit
does not modify
\mathcal{P}
. Let
\ell_{\mathsf{next}}
denote the layer at which we jump to.
Refit
made no updates to
\widehat{M}_{\mathsf{lat}}
at layers
\ell_{\mathsf{next}}+1
onwards, and therefore the previously computed test policies
\Pi^{\mathsf{test}}_{\ell_{\mathsf{next}}+1:H}
must still be valid, so therefore properties (2) and (3) continue to hold at layer
\ell_{\mathsf{next}}
onwards.

Continuing the induction, once
\ell\leftarrow 0
is reached in
PLHR
, the policy emulator
\widehat{M}
satisfies the bound

\displaystyle\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\penalty\ \left\lvert V^{\pi}(s_{1})-\En_{x_{1}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\mathcal{X}_{1}[\widehat{M}])}[\widehat{V}^{\pi}(x_{1})]\right\rvert\leq\Gamma_{1}\leq\widetilde{O}(SAH^{2}\varepsilon).

(59)

Bounding the Number of Calls to
Decoder
and
Refit
.

By
Lemma
38
, the total number of executions of
line
15
in
Algorithm
6
is at most
(n_{\mathsf{reset}}AH/\varepsilon^{2})\cdot\log n_{\mathsf{reset}}
. In the worst case, every revisit to an already computed layer (i.e., jumping back to
\ell_{\mathsf{next}}\geq\ell
) requires us to restart
Decoder
at layer
H
and therefore decode at most
n_{\mathsf{reset}}AH
additional times, so therefore

\displaystyle T_{\mathsf{D}}\leq\frac{n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{2}A^{2}H^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log n_{\mathsf{reset}}.

Similarly, every revisit in the worst case requires
H
additional calls to
Refit
so therefore

\displaystyle T_{\mathsf{R}}\leq\frac{n_{\mathsf{reset}}AH^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\log n_{\mathsf{reset}}.

As claimed, both
T_{\mathsf{D}}
and
T_{\mathsf{R}}
are
\mathrm{poly}(C_{\mathsf{push}},S,A,H,\varepsilon^{-1},\log\lvert\Pi\rvert,\log\delta^{-1})
.

Final Sample Complexity Bound.

Now we compute the total number of samples.

•

Algorithm
4
uses
n_{\mathsf{reset}}\cdot AH
samples to
\mu_{h}
to form the state space of the policy emulator, and for each state-action pair we sample
\widetilde{O}(H^{2}\varepsilon^{-2})
times to estimate the reward.

•

Algorithm
5
is called
T_{\mathsf{D}}\leq\widetilde{O}(n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{2}A^{2}H^{2}\varepsilon^{-2})
times, and each time we use
n_{\mathsf{dec}}\cdot n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{2}n_{\mathsf{mc}}
rollouts.

•

Algorithm
6
is called
T_{\mathsf{R}}\leq\widetilde{O}(n_{\mathsf{reset}}AH^{2}\varepsilon^{-2})
times, and each time we use
2n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{2}n_{\mathsf{mc}}
to evaluate the test policies. Furthermore, by
Lemma
38
,
line
11
is triggered at most
\widetilde{O}(n_{\mathsf{reset}}AH\varepsilon^{-2})
times, with every time requiring an additional
n_{\mathsf{mc}}\cdot n_{\mathsf{reset}}AH
rollouts.

Therefore in total we use

\displaystyle\hskip-20.00003ptn_{\mathsf{reset}}\frac{AH^{3}}{\varepsilon^{2}}+n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{4}n_{\mathsf{dec}}n_{\mathsf{mc}}\frac{A^{2}H^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}+n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{3}n_{\mathsf{mc}}\frac{AH^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}+n_{\mathsf{reset}}^{2}n_{\mathsf{mc}}\frac{A^{2}H^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{\mathsf{push}}^{4}S^{6}A^{12}H^{3}}{\varepsilon^{18}}\cdot\mathrm{polylog}\left(C_{\mathsf{push}},S,A,H,\lvert\Pi\rvert,\varepsilon^{-1},\delta^{-1}\right)\quad\text{samples.}

Afterwards, we can rescale
\varepsilon\leftarrow\varepsilon/\widetilde{O}(SAH^{2})
so that the bound Eq. (
59
) is at most
\varepsilon
, and rescale
\delta\leftarrow\delta/(3T_{\mathsf{D}}+T_{\mathsf{R}}+1)
so that the guarantee occurs with probability at least
1-\delta
. The final sample complexity is

\displaystyle\frac{C_{\mathsf{push}}^{4}S^{24}A^{30}H^{39}}{\varepsilon^{18}}\cdot\mathrm{polylog}\left(C_{\mathsf{push}},S,A,H,\lvert\Pi\rvert,\varepsilon^{-1},\delta^{-1}\right)\quad\text{samples.}

∎
